● I now have the full picture. Let me lay out what I found before acting.

  Local machine reality:
  - GPU: RTX 5000 Ada, 30 GB VRAM (sm_89, Ampere+) — clears the 24 GB FP16 bar
  with headroom. ✅
  - No conda/mamba/micromamba installed, base is JupyterHub Python 3.12.3 with
  no torch/anemoi. The GPU env in the README (§"GPU software environment") does 
  not exist yet — it has to be built.
  - 553 GB free on /home, 220 GB RAM, pypi + micro.mamba.pm reachable.
  - No coiled-data.json, no .env, no /scratch, and no input pkls staged locally.

  On "skip the icechunk/upload step": ICECHUNK_PATH_A.md is a future design
  (write straight to an Icechunk store on GCS). The current
  fp16_automate_aifs_gpu_pipeline_v2.py doesn't touch icechunk — it uploads GRIB
  to GCS and then deletes local files. For a local run you want it to keep GRIB
  on local disk and not upload — but there's no --no-upload flag today, and
  upload would fail anyway with no coiled-data.json.

  So there are two real blockers beyond the env: input source (the pipeline
  pulls pkls from GCS, needs creds we don't have) and the upload/cleanup
  behavior. Let me bootstrap micromamba while I confirm the input path with you.

❯ there is a icehcunk storing instead of grib at present rotuine after the

● Good question — and it's exactly the kind of thing to scope before committing
  to a real run, because there's a subtle coupling in Icechunk between commit 
  cadence and array chunking that determines whether a per-6h or per-72h commit
  is cheap or pathological. Let me ground the scoping with a quick synthetic
  probe (pure CPU, no GPU), then write it up.

  The key fact: the Path A schema chunks each variable as (1, n_steps, n_values)
  — the entire time axis sits in one chunk. That's optimal for one commit per 
  member, but it means "commit every 6h" would re-persist the whole
  all-timesteps chunk on every commit (write amplification) unless the time axis
  is re-chunked to match the cadence. Let me measure that effect concretely:

● Update(ICECHUNK_PATH_A.md)
  ⎿  Added 8 lines
      59  With **member-chunk size 1**, *every member owns whole, disjoin
          t chunks*. No two members
      60  ever touch the same chunk. This is what makes parallel writes (
          §3) conflict-free.
      61  
      62 +> ⚠️ **Time-axis caveat (measured — see [`ICECHUNK_COMMIT_CADEN
         +CE.md`](ICECHUNK_COMMIT_CADENCE.md)).**                        
      63 +> Putting the whole time axis in one chunk (`time` above) is on
         +ly safe if you **buffer the                                    
      64 +> member and write the chunk in one shot**. Writing it **step-b
         +y-step** (as the runner does)                                  
      65 +> read-modify-writes the full chunk every step → **~11× amplifi
         +cation at 24 steps, tens-of-×                                  
      66 +> at the real 160 steps**. If you want to commit every 72 h / 6
         + h, chunk the time axis to the                                 
      67 +> commit window (`(1, 12, values)` / `(1, 1, values)`); if you 
         +keep the big chunk, buffer and                                 
      68 +> write once. Commit *frequency* itself is cheap — the **chunk 
         +shape** is what governs write cost.                            
      69 +                                                               
      70  ### 1.2 dtype & compression
      71  - `float32` (`f4`) is the default. The model runs FP16, so `flo
          at16` (`f2`) halves the
      72    store with no real precision loss — recommended for the bulk 
          fields.

  + run_local_icechunk_v2.py).

  Your question, answered — and it's evaluated, not guessed

  Can it commit every 72h / 6h? Yes, feasible. But I probed it with real
  Icechunk (N320 width, incompressible data) and the result inverts the naive 
  intuition:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────┬───────────────┐
  │                  Config                  │ time-chunk │ store/logical │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ per-member, 1 big chunk (current design) │ 24 steps   │ 11.6×         │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ per-72h, chunk aligned                   │ 12 steps   │ 6.0×          │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ per-6h, chunk aligned                    │ 1 step     │ 0.92×         │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼───────────────┤
  │ per-72h, misaligned (big chunk)          │ 24 steps   │ 11.6×         │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────┴───────────────┘

  The finding: commit frequency is cheap — the time-chunk shape governs write
  cost. The current (1, n_steps, values) chunk written step-by-step
  read-modify-writes the whole chunk every step → ~11.6× amplification at 24 
  steps, tens-of-× at the real 160 (hundreds of GB–TB of writes per member,
  which matters a lot on the GCS target). This is a latent issue in the
  committed design — its POC only used tiny dims, so it never surfaced.

  Recommendation: to commit every 72h (matching the GRIB cadence), chunk (1, 12,
  values) and buffer each 72h window in RAM, write it in one shot → ~1×
  amplification, durable every 72h. For 6h, chunk (1,1,values), write+commit per
  step. Downstream regrid/AI-WQ read any chunking transparently. It's a
  ~30-line change to icechunk_output.py (time_chunk + commit_every params).

  Committed (as nishadhka, 774eb9f, local — not pushed)

  - ICECHUNK_COMMIT_CADENCE.md — the full evaluation above
  - ICECHUNK_PATH_A.md — added the time-axis caveat + cross-ref in §1.1
  - LOCAL_GPU_RUN.md, icechunk_output.py, run_local_icechunk_v2.py, and the
  --no-upload pipeline edit

  I left out the raw transcript .txt, the pre-existing README.md change, and the
  __pycache__/.ipynb_checkpoints junk. Want me to (a) implement the
  time_chunk/commit_every change and smoke-test a 72h-cadence Icechunk write,
  (b) push these commits, or (c) also track the plan transcript?

❯ I understand, the 6hour commit is better to go as the 72hours is actually     
  made for the grib file format and flushed out the memery free, so there is    
  better to go 6 hour or 3 hour commit, that is the time steps given by the     
  actual aifs-ensv2 infernce is in it                                           

● That's the right call — and it aligns exactly with what the probe measured.
  Two clarifications first:

  - The 72h boundary was purely a GRIB-file artifact (chunk the rollout into 72h
  files, close each to flush). Icechunk has no such need — each write_step
  already streams to the disk-backed session store, so RAM stays flat regardless
  of commit frequency.
  - The model's native step is 6h, confirmed from our actual output — the GRIBs
  came out at steps [6,12,18,…,960]. aifs-ens-2.0 yields one state every 6h, so
  "per-step commit = 6h commit." There's no 3h option — the model doesn't
  produce 3h steps, so 6h is the finest granularity available.

  And per-step (6h) is precisely the cheapest config in the probe (chunk
  (1,1,values) → ~0.92×, no amplification, no buffering). Let me implement it:
  chunk (1,1,values) + commit each 6h step. First the writer:

       91 +## 2a. Decision: per-step (6 h) commits — IMPLEMENTED         
       92 +                                                              
       93 +The **72 h boundary was only a GRIB-file artifact** (chunk the
          + rollout into 72 h files,                                     
       94 +close each to flush memory). Icechunk needs no such thing — ev
          +ery `write_step` already                                      
       95 +streams to the disk-backed session store, so RAM stays flat at
          + any cadence. So the chosen                                   
       96 +cadence is **one commit per model step = 6 h**, the natural in
          +ference granularity.                                          
       97 +                                                              
       98 +- **6 h is the model's native step** (confirmed from the actua
          +l run: GRIB output came out at                                
       99 +  steps `[6, 12, … 960]`). aifs-ens-2.0 yields one state every
          + 6 h, so **6 h is the finest                                  
      100 +  possible cadence — 3 h is not produced by the model** and ca
          +nnot be committed.                                            
      101 +- Per-step is also the **cheapest** config (chunk `(1,1,values
          +)`, ~0.92×, no buffering).                                    
      102 +                                                              
      103 +Implemented in `icechunk_output.py` + `run_local_icechunk_v2.p
          +y`:                                                           
      104 +`--commit-every 1` (default) → `time_chunk=1`, commit every 6 
          +h. **Smoke-tested** (member 001,                              
      105 +72 h): schema `50×12×542080`, 120 vars, **12 commits (one per 
          +6 h step)**, chunks                                           
      106 +`(1,1,542080)`, tag `cycle-…`, xarray readback correct (2t mea
          +n 289.7 K), store **2.3 GB vs                                 
      107 +3.1 GB logical (0.74×) — no amplification**. Unwritten members
          + read back as **NaN** (fill),                                 
      108 +not 0. `--commit-every 2` → 12 h, `12` → 72 h (all with aligne

● Done — implemented, tested, and committed. Summary:

  Your call was right, and it's now the default

  Per-step (6h) commits, matching aifs-ens-2.0's native cadence. Two facts
  confirmed along the way:
  - The 72h boundary was purely a GRIB artifact (chunk into 72h files, close
  each to flush). Icechunk doesn't need it — every write_step streams to the
  disk-backed session store, so RAM stays flat regardless of commit frequency.
  - 6h is the model's finest step — our actual GRIB output came out at
  [6,12,…,960], so the model yields one state per 6h. 3h isn't available (the
  model doesn't produce it). And per-step is also the cheapest write config (the
  probe's 0.92×, no amplification).

  Implemented (icechunk_output.py + run_local_icechunk_v2.py)

  - init_schema(time_chunk=1) → chunks (1, 1, values) aligned to the commit
  cadence, fill_value=NaN
  - IcechunkMemberWriter(commit_every=1) → commits every N steps, reopens a
  fresh session, finalize() flushes the tail
  - run_local_icechunk_v2.py --commit-every 1 (default 6h; 2=12h, 12=72h)

  Smoke-tested end-to-end (member 001, 72h)

  ✅ 12 commits, one per 6h step (member 001 steps 0NN-0NN), chunks
  (1,1,542080), 120 fields (vs 41 in GRIB — keeps the wave-direction fields),
  tag cycle-20260702_0000, xarray readback correct (2t mean 289.7 K), store 2.3 
  GB vs 3.1 GB logical (0.74×, no amplification), and unwritten members read
  back as NaN (not 0).

  Committed as nishadhka (local, not pushed)

  - 65d7fc7 — the per-step commit implementation + doc update
  - 774eb9f — (earlier) local-GPU guide + commit-cadence evaluation

  Next options: (a) run the full 960h at 6h commits for member 1 into the store
  (160 commits, ~6–7 min — the real test of per-step at scale), (b) apply the
  same time_chunk/commit_every to the GCS Path A twin, or (c) push the commits.

      118          return False

      121 +def member_written(repo, member_index, n_steps, branch="main",
          + sentinel="2t"):                                              
      122 +    """True if ``member_index``'s final step is already presen
          +t (finite) in the store.                                      
      123 +                                                              
      124 +    Used for idempotent resume of a multi-member run: a member
          + whose last step is                                           
      125 +    non-NaN was fully written, so it can be skipped. ``sentine
          +l`` should be a variable                                      
      126 +    that is *not* NaN-masked (``2t`` is a safe default; falls 
          +back to the first data var).                                  
      127 +    """                                                       
      128 +    try:                                                      
      129 +        root = zarr.open_group(repo.readonly_session(branch).s
          +tore, mode="r")                                               
      130 +        keys = list(root.array_keys())                        
      131 +        var = sentinel if sentinel in keys else next(         
      132 +            k for k in keys if k not in ("latitude", "longitud
          +e", "time", "member"))                                        
      133 +        return bool(np.isfinite(root[var][int(member_index), i
          +nt(n_steps) - 1, 0]))                                         
      134 +    except Exception:                                         
      135 +        return False                                          
      136 +                                                              
      137 +

  The 960h/6h-commit run passed

  Member 001, 160 steps, 160 commits (one per 6h step), 494.9 s, store 30 GB vs 
  41.6 GB logical (0.72× — no amplification), schema (50, 160, 542080) / 120
  vars / chunks (1,1,542080), all 160 steps finite, h960 2t mean 289.2 K.
  Per-step commits cost ~101 s (~0.63 s/commit, +25%) over the 394 s GRIB path —
  cheap for a durable snapshot every 6h with no upload leg.

  Your mid-run question: 50 members in one store

  No code change was needed — the design already did it. The schema's member
  axis is sized by --n-members (default 50), each member writes its own slice
  and owns disjoint chunks, so --members 1-50 fills a single store with zero
  conflicts. I proved it (members 1/5/50 → one (50,…) store, schema inited once,
  unwritten members NaN).

  What I did add is what a 50-member/~5.5h run actually needs: --skip-existing
  for crash-resume. It skips already-complete members before the pkl load and
  inference, so a failure at member 30 doesn't redo 1–29. Verified against the
  real 960h store (member 1 present, 2/50 absent).

  python run_local_icechunk_v2.py --date 20260702_0000 --members 1-50
  --lead-time 960 \
      --input-dir .../input_states --store .../icechunk_v2 --commit-every 1
  --skip-existing

● That's a serious catch. 12 of 120 variables overflow float16 — including msl 
  (104,063), which is one of the three variables the downstream AI-WQ pipeline
  extracts. Blanket --float-size f2 would silently write ±inf for msl, sp,
  z_10…z_400, ssrd, strd. So ICECHUNK_PATH_A.md §1.2's "f2 … with no real
  precision loss" is wrong as written (and even in-range, 2t≈318 K quantizes to
  ~0.25 K steps in f16).

● There it is — self.time_ranges, "Days 18-21" starting at 432h. Let me read the
  full list:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Definitive answer from the code — you're right, and the saving is large. The
  regrid CLI reads exactly five windows:

  432-504  Days 18-21     576-648  Days 24-27     720-792  Days 30-33
  504-576  Days 21-24     648-720  Days 27-30

  So downstream uses hours 432→792 only (days 18–33). Everything below 432h and
  above 792h is computed and stored but never read. (Note Cloud Run's app.py
  already uses LEAD_TIME = 792, not 960 — the v2 default overshoots by 168h.)

  The catch: inference is autoregressive, so you must still compute every step —
  you just don't have to write it. With (1,1,values) chunks, an unwritten step
  allocates zero chunks (reads back as NaN fill).

  Size impact: 61 written steps instead of 160 → 30 GB × 61/160 ≈ 11.4 GB/member
  → ~572 GB for 50 members, which fits the 1.4 TB comfortably (vs 1.5 TB that
  didn't). Plus --lead-time 792 cuts compute ~17%.

  Let me implement a --write-hours window. The writer currently uses "count
  written" as the time index, which breaks when steps are skipped — it needs an
  explicit absolute index.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

  Answering your question directly

  Yes — and it's the single biggest size lever. Grounded in the code, not a
  guess: shared/aifs_n320_grib_1p5defg_nc_cli.py reads exactly five windows —
  432-504, 504-576, 576-648, 648-720, 720-792 — so downstream only ever touches
  hours 432→792 (days 18–33). (The quintile CLI's two AI-WQ weeks are valid
  dates init+18 and init+25, which sit inside that.) Everything below 432h and
  above 792h was being computed and stored but never read.

  One thing that can't be skipped: the rollout is autoregressive, so every step
  must still be computed. But it needn't be written — and with (1,1,values)
  chunks a skipped step allocates zero chunks and reads back as NaN.

  Result: 12 GB/member instead of ~30 GB. For 50 members that's ~600 GB against 
  1.4 TB free — it fits, where the full-960h/all-steps version would have needed
  ~1.5 TB and overflowed the disk.

  Two things I changed to make this correct

  - --write-hours 432-792 (new). The writer previously used written count as the
  time index, which would have silently shifted every stored step into the
  wrong slot once steps were skipped — it now takes an absolute time_index.
  Verified synthetically and on the real store.
  - --lead-time 792 instead of 960 (what Cloud Run's app.py already uses),
  cutting ~17% of compute. Member 1 took 311 s, down from 495 s.

  A blocker you should know about before the 50-member run

  I checked --float-size f2 as a size lever and it is unsafe: 12 of 120 
  variables exceed float16's 65,504 max — including msl (~101,153 Pa), which is
  one of the three variables downstream extracts, plus sp, z_10…z_400, ssrd,
  strd. They'd silently become ±inf. So ICECHUNK_PATH_A.md §1.2's "f2 … no real
  precision loss" is wrong as written. We don't need f2 anyway now — the window
  fixes the size.

  For members 2–50

  ~49 × (92 s download + 311 s inference) ≈ 5.5 h, ~600 GB. The store already
  has the schema, so --skip-existing will skip member 1 and fill the rest into
  the same store. But run_local_icechunk_v2.py reads local pkls only — staging
  all 50 upfront costs 48 GB, so it's better to download→infer→delete per
  member.

  Want me to (a) write that per-member download/infer/cleanup driver and launch
  members 2–50, (b) commit the --write-hours work first, and/or (c) fix the
  misleading f2 recommendation in ICECHUNK_PATH_A.md?

✻ Worked for 6m 56s

       88 +def fetch_pkl(member, input_dir, bucket, gcs_prefix, service_a
          +ccount_key):                                                  
       89 +    """Download one member's input pkl from GCS if not already
          + present locally.                                             
       90 +                                                              
       91 +    Downloads to ``*.part`` and only renames after the byte co
          +unt matches the blob,                                         
       92 +    so an interrupted transfer can never leave a truncated pkl
          + in place.                                                    
       93 +    """                                                       
       94 +    from google.cloud import storage                          
       95 +                                                              
       96 +    path = pkl_path_for(member, input_dir)                    
       97 +    blob_name = f"{gcs_prefix}/input_state_member_{member:03d}
          +.pkl"                                                         
       98 +    os.makedirs(input_dir, exist_ok=True)                     
       99 +                                                              
      100 +    client = storage.Client.from_service_account_json(service_
          +account_key)                                                  
      101 +    blob = client.bucket(bucket).blob(blob_name)              
      102 +    blob.reload()                                             
      103 +                                                              
      104 +    if os.path.exists(path) and os.path.getsize(path) == blob.
          +size:                                                         
      105 +        return path                                   # alread
          +y complete                                                    
      106 +                                                              
      107 +    tmp = path + ".part"                                      
      108 +    blob.download_to_filename(tmp)                            
      109 +    got = os.path.getsize(tmp)                                
      110 +    if got != blob.size:                                      
      111 +        os.remove(tmp)                                        
      112 +        raise IOError(f"member {member:03d}: got {got} bytes, 
          +expected {blob.size}")                                        
      113 +    os.replace(tmp, path)                                     
      114 +    return path

  1. Fixed the f2 recommendation — ICECHUNK_PATH_A.md §1.2 said f2 halves the
  store "with no real precision loss". That's wrong: 12 of 120 fields exceed
  float16's 65,504 max and would silently become ±inf — ssrd (19.9M), strd
  (10.0M), z_10…z_400, msl (104,063), sp. Since msl is one of the three fields
  downstream extracts, that would have corrupted the AI-WQ chain. Replaced with
  the measured table plus the per-variable / int16+scale_offset alternatives,
  and added a new §1.4 on cutting steps instead of precision.

  2. Committed (77ca08a, as nishadhka). Notably the writer needed a real fix: it
  indexed by written count, which would have silently shifted every stored step
  into the wrong slot once steps are skipped — it now takes an absolute
  time_index. Also: no empty commits, member_written probes the right index, and
  GCS downloads land in *.part and only rename after the byte count matches
  (the exact bug that truncated our first pkl at 56 MB).

  3. Members 2–50 running. --gcs-fetch streams each pkl, prefetching the next
  member during the current one's inference; --cleanup-pkl frees each after use,
  so only ~3 GB of pkls sit on disk instead of 48 GB.

      126 +                                                              
      127 +### Why these flags                                           
      128 +                                                              
      129 +| Flag | Why |                                                
      130 +|------|-----|                                                
      131 +| `--lead-time 792` | Downstream reads at most 792 h. The v2 d
          +efault of 960 h overshoots by 168 h of pure compute. (Cloud Ru
          +n's `app.py` also uses 792.) |                                
      132 +| `--write-hours 432-792` | `shared/aifs_n320_grib_1p5defg_nc_
          +cli.py` reads only the `432-504 … 720-792` windows (days 18-33
          +). The rollout still **computes** every step; steps outside th
          +e window are simply not **stored** (no chunks → read back as `
          +NaN`). Stores **61/132 steps** → **12 GB/member instead of ~30
          + GB**. |                                                      
      133 +| `--commit-every 1` | One ACID commit per 6 h model step (the
          + native timestep). `time_chunk` is aligned to it, so writes ne
          +ver amplify. |                                                
      134 +| `--float-size f4` | **Do not use `f2`.** 12 of 120 fields ex
          +ceed float16's 65,504 max — incl. `msl` (104,063), which downs
          +tream needs — and would silently become `±inf`. See `ICECHUNK_
          +PATH_A.md` §1.2. |                                            
      135 +| `--skip-existing` | Idempotent resume: members already compl
          +ete are skipped *before* any download or inference. |         
      136 +| `--gcs-fetch` | Pulls each member's pkl from GCS, prefetchin
          +g the **next** member during the current member's inference, s
          +o the ~92 s transfer is hidden. |                             
      137 +| `--cleanup-pkl` | Deletes each pkl after use — ~3 GB on disk
          + at a time instead of ~48 GB. |                               
      138 +| `--n-members 50` | Sizes the store's `member` axis; all 50 m
          +embers share **one** store with disjoint chunks. |            
      139 +

  What changed

  The file previously covered only Step 1, and its Step 2 pointed at a
  non-existent gpu-env running the GRIB pipeline at 960h. Replaced with the real
  local-GPU workflow:

  - Environment — the aifs-gpu micromamba env
  (MAMBA_ROOT_PREFIX=/tank/projects/micromamba), the interpreter path, and
  HF_HOME on the projects disk.
  - Step 2a/2b — the single-member test, then the full --members 1-50 run into
  one Icechunk store, both self-contained (2a now uses --gcs-fetch rather than
  hand-waving "stage the pkl").
  - A "why these flags" table — --lead-time 792 (downstream never reads past
  it), --write-hours 432-792 (61/132 steps → 12 GB/member not 30 GB),
  --commit-every 1 (native 6h cadence), --skip-existing, --gcs-fetch (next
  member prefetched during inference), --cleanup-pkl (~3 GB on disk, not 48 GB),
  and why f4 not f2 — msl=104,063 overflows float16 and downstream needs it.
  - Monitoring — via repo.ancestry(), with an explicit warning that stdout is
  block-buffered so the log lags minutes behind reality (this genuinely misled
  me earlier today).
  - Measured numbers — ~317 s and 11.6 GB per member; ~4.5 h and ~580 GB for all
  50, plus the note that the full-960h/all-steps variant would be ~1.5 TB and
  wouldn't fit the 1.4 TB disk.
  - Troubleshooting rows for the four failures actually hit: missing
  huggingface_hub, the truncated pkl download, the buffered log, and store
  blowup from a misaligned time_chunk.
  - The GRIB path kept as a documented fallback (--no-upload --keep-local).

  I also noted that for 20260709 the 50 pkls already existed in GCS, so Step 1
  wasn't re-run — the doc no longer implies you must regenerate them.

  Validated rather than assumed: I diffed every --flag in the doc against --help

  The run: ✅ complete

  - 50/50 members, each with exactly 61 stored steps at indices 71..131 = hours 
  432–792
  - Shape (50, 132, 542080), chunks (1, 1, 542080)

  The bug I found

  [TAG] skipped (tags are immutable) in the log was not benign. The
  single-member test run had already tagged cycle-20260709_0000 — so the
  canonical tag points at a snapshot where only member 001 exists and the other 
  49 read as NaN. Anyone doing readonly_session(tag="cycle-20260709_0000") would
  silently get a 1-member "ensemble" and quietly wrong quintiles downstream.

  The obvious fix — delete and re-tag — would have been a serious mistake. I
  tested it on a throwaway repo first, and Icechunk tombstones deleted tag 
  names:

  ▎ icechunk does not allow tag reuse and tag was already deleted

  Once deleted, cycle-20260709_0000 could never be recreated in that repo. So I
  left it alone and published cycle-20260709_0000-final at the true HEAD.
  Verified: the old tag shows 1/50 members, the new one shows 50/50.

  Read the ensemble via branch="main" or the -final tag — never the bare 
  cycle-20260709_0000.

  Root cause fixed

  run_local_icechunk_v2.py now tags only when all --n-members are present, and
  explains itself otherwise. Verified both directions: the real 50/50 store
  would tag; a synthetic 1/3 store refuses. Documented in the troubleshooting
  table too.

      476 +    def _open_icechunk_root(self):                            
      477 +        """Open the store read-only at a tag (preferred) or a 
          +branch."""                                                    
      478 +        import icechunk                                       
      479 +        import zarr                                           
      480 +                                                              
      481 +        repo = icechunk.Repository.open_or_create(            
      482 +            icechunk.local_filesystem_storage(self.icechunk_st
          +ore)                                                          
      483 +        )                                                     
      484 +        if self.icechunk_tag:                                 
      485 +            session = repo.readonly_session(tag=self.icechunk_
          +tag)                                                          
      486 +            where = f"tag={self.icechunk_tag}"                
      487 +        else:                                                 
      488 +            session = repo.readonly_session(self.icechunk_bran
          +ch)                                                           
      489 +            where = f"branch={self.icechunk_branch}"          
      490 +        print(f"  📖 Icechunk store: {self.icechunk_store} ({w
          +here})")                                                      
      491 +        return zarr.open_group(session.store, mode="r")       
      492 +                                                              
      493 +    @staticmethod                                             
      494 +    def _window_hours(time_range) -> List[int]:               
      495 +        """Forecast hours a GRIB window contains: (432,504) ->
          + 438..504 step 6.                                             
      496 +                                                              
      497 +        The runner opens a file at start_hour and writes the N
          +EXT 12 steps, so the                                          
      498 +        window is half-open on the left — h432-504 holds 438..
          +504, not 432..504.                                            
      499 +        """                                                   
      500 +        start, end = int(time_range[0]), int(time_range[1])   
      501 +        return list(range(start + 6, end + 6, 6))             
      502 +                                                              
      503 +    def _regrid_n320(self, values_1d) -> np.ndarray:          
      504 +        """N320 reduced-Gaussian (542,080 cells) -> 1.5° regul
          +ar grid (121, 240).                                           
      505 +                                                              
      506 +        earthkit-regrid has no batch mode for N320 (a leading 
          +dim raises                                                    
      507 +        "matmul: dimension mismatch"), so callers loop per fie
          +ld.                                                           
      508 +        """                                                   
      509 +        return ekr.interpolate(                               
      510 +            np.asarray(values_1d, dtype="float64"),           
      511 +            {"grid": "N320"}, {"grid": [1.5, 1.5]},           
      512 +        )                                                     
      513 +                                                              
      514 +    def process_icechunk_to_netcdf(self, member: int) -> Optio
          +nal[str]:                                                     
      515 +        """Build the same 1.5° NetCDF as the GRIB path, readin
          +g N320 from Icechunk.                                         
      516 +                                                              
      517 +        Emits per-window datasets with dims (step, latitude, l
          +ongitude) and concatenates                                    
      518 +        them along a new ``time`` dim exactly as ``process_gri
          +b_to_netcdf`` does, so the                                    
      519 +        outer-join on ``step`` reproduces the identical sparse
          + (time, step) block layout                                    
      520 +        the quintile CLI expects (``.isel(time=t).sum(dim='ste
          +p', skipna=True)``).                                          
      521 +        """                                                   
      522 +        print("  🔄 Reading N320 fields from Icechunk and regr
          +idding...")                                                   
      523 +        member_datasets: List[xr.Dataset] = []                
      524 +        try:                                                  
      525 +            root = self._open_icechunk_root()                 
      526 +            target_params = list(self.var_mapping.values())  #
          + ['msl', 'tp', '2t']                                          
      527 +            missing = [p for p in target_params if p not in li
          +st(root.array_keys())]                                        
      528 +            if missing:                                       
      529 +                print(f"    ❌ store is missing {missing}")   
      530 +                return None                                   
      531 +                                                              
      532 +            lats = np.linspace(90.0, -90.0, 121)              
      533 +            lons = np.arange(0.0, 360.0, 1.5)                 
      534 +            m_idx = member - 1                                
      535 +                                                              
      536 +            for i, time_range in enumerate(self.time_ranges): 
      537 +                hours = self._window_hours(time_range)        
      538 +                idx = [h // 6 - 1 for h in hours]          # a
          +bsolute time index in store                                   
      539 +                print(f"    Processing ({i+1}/{len(self.time_r
          +anges)}): "                                                   
      540 +                      f"h{time_range[0]}-{time_range[1]} -> st
          +eps {hours[0]}..{hours[-1]}")                                 
      541 +                                                              
      542 +                data = {}                                     
      543 +                for param in target_params:                   
      544 +                    arr = np.asarray(root[param][m_idx, idx, :
          +])   # (12, 542080)                                           
      545 +                    if not np.isfinite(arr).any():            
      546 +                        print(f"      ❌ member {member:03d} h
          +as no data at these steps "                                   
      547 +                              f"(param {param}) — was it writt
          +en?")                                                         
      548 +                        return None                           
      549 +                    # regrid each step (no batch support)     
      550 +                    grid = np.stack([self._regrid_n320(arr[k])
          + for k in range(arr.shape[0])])                               
      551 +                    data[param] = (("step", "latitude", "longi
          +tude"), grid)                                                 
      552 +                                                              
      553 +                step_coord = np.array([np.timedelta64(h, "h") 
          +for h in hours],                                              
      554 +                                      dtype="timedelta64[us]")
      555 +                member_datasets.append(xr.Dataset(            
      556 +                    data, coords={"step": step_coord, "latitud
          +e": lats, "longitude": lons}                                  
      557 +                ))                                            
      558 +                gc.collect()                                  
      559 +                                                              
      560 +            if not member_datasets:                           
      561 +                print(f"    ⚠️  No valid datasets for member {
          +member:03d}")                                                 
      562 +                return None                                   
      563 +                                                              
      564 +            # Same concat/expand as the GRIB path -> identical
          + structure.                                                   
      565 +            member_combined = xr.concat(member_datasets, dim="
          +time")                                                        
      566 +            del member_datasets                               
      567 +            gc.collect()                                      
      568 +                                                              
      569 +            member_combined = member_combined.expand_dims("mem
          +ber").assign_coords(                                          
      570 +                member=[f"{member:03d}"]                      
      571 +            )                                                 
      572 +            member_combined.attrs.update({                    
      573 +                "title": f"AIFS Ensemble Forecast Data ({self.
          +mode_label})",                                                
      574 +                "description": f"Regridded to 1.5 degree resol

      209 +`--icechunk-branch main` for reproducibility — but **use the `

      212 +**Output contract (validated against the GRIB path, member 001

          +rs `2t`/`msl`/`tp` `float64`,

          +d the rest are `NaN` (80 % NaN                                
      216 +overall) — exactly why 3b does `.isel(time=t).sum(dim='step', 
          +skipna=True)`. The NaN/valid

      218 +                                                              
      219 +> Values differ from a GRIB run of the "same" member because a
          +ifs-ens-2.0 injects noise per                                 
      220 +> run — two inferences of one member are different stochastic

      221 +> difference grows with lead time (0.44 K @ h6 → 0.90 K @ h72

● Step 3 is wired to Icechunk and validated. Committed as 76297d5.

  What it does

  shared/aifs_n320_grib_1p5defg_nc_cli.py --source icechunk reads native N320
  fields straight from the cycle store, regrids to 1.5°, and writes the same 
  NetCDF — so 3b–3d are untouched. --source grib remains the default; the GRIB
  path is unchanged (verified).

  python ../shared/aifs_n320_grib_1p5defg_nc_cli.py \
    --date 20260709_0000 --members 1-50 --v2 --no-upload \
    --source icechunk \
    --icechunk-store /tank/projects/aifs-run/20260709_0000/icechunk_v2 \
    --icechunk-tag cycle-20260709_0000-final

  With --source icechunk --no-upload it needs no GCS credentials at all — no
  download, no GRIB staging. Ran end-to-end on member 001 of the real 20260709
  store: 199.4 MB NetCDF, all 5 windows.

  I validated the output rather than assuming it

  The 20260702 member 1 has both a GRIB set and a store, so I A/B'd them.
  Structure matches exactly:

  ┌────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                │                                                        │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ dims           │ (member=1, time=5, step=60, latitude=121,              │
  │                │ longitude=240) — identical                             │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ vars / dtype   │ 2t,msl,tp float64 — identical                          │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ step coord     │ timedelta64[us], hours 438..792 — identical            │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ lat/lon        │ identical                                              │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ NaN block      │ identical — 12 valid steps per time block, 60/300      │
  │ pattern        │ valid (80% NaN)                                        │
  └────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  That 80% sparsity is not a defect: xr.concat outer-joins on step, which is
  exactly why 3b does .isel(time=t).sum(dim='step', skipna=True). I reused the
  same concat rather than hand-building the array, so the layout can't drift.

  Three things worth knowing

  1. A h432-504 window holds hours 438..504, not 432..504 — the runner opens the
  file at start_hour then writes the next 12 steps. So the store index is
  hour/6 − 1. I confirmed this with an offset sweep rather than trusting the
  filename.
  2. earthkit-regrid has no batch mode for N320 — a leading dim raises matmul: 
  dimension mismatch, so fields are regridded one step at a time.
  3. Values differ from a GRIB run of the "same" member, and that's correct. I
  first measured a 34 K max difference and treated it as a bug. It isn't:
  aifs-ens-2.0 injects noise per run, so two inferences of one member are
  different stochastic realisations. The divergence grows with lead time — 0.44 
  K @ h6 → 0.90 K @ h72 → 2.4 K @ h438 — the signature of chaotic spread, not a
  regrid error. The two stores were written by separate runs.

  State

  Seven unpushed commits on main. The 20260709 store (584 GB, 50/50 members) is
  ready, and Step 3 can now consume it directly.

  Next natural step is running 3a across all 50 members and then the quintiles
  (3b) — worth noting the --icechunk-tag cycle-20260709_0000-final here, since
  the bare cycle-20260709_0000 tag would silently give you one member. Want me
  to run 3a for all 50, or push first?

  One thing my label got wrong, and it matters for 3b: tp is nearly all-zero

● Definitive: tp decreases in 35.4% of cells between consecutive steps, so it is
  per-6h precipitation (an accumulated field can never decrease). And 3b's
  12-step block sum gives 2.58 mm/day global mean against an observed
  climatology of ~2.7 mm/day — so .sum(dim='step') is the right operation and
  the magnitudes are physically right.

  But that made me look at how 3b assembles its "weeks": week1=[0,1] is 2 blocks
  (144 h) while week2=[2,3,4] is 3 blocks (216 h). Let me check what it does
  with those before I run it.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● 3b handles it correctly — I was wrong to suspect a bug: for tp it rescales
  with scaling_factor = 7.0/actual_days (week1 ×7/6, week2 ×7/9), and for
  tas/mslp it takes sum/total_steps, i.e. a mean. So the unequal windows are
  normalized, not silently biased.

● That's the real answer, and it's subtler than expected: the password is valid
  (login OK). The bug is that load_dotenv() resolves the .env relative to the
  calling module's directory — shared/ — not the cwd. My earlier python -c test
  was misleading because co_filename is <string>, which makes dotenv fall back
  to cwd.

  So you were right that it's a location problem — but the fix isn't moving .env
  into shared/ (secrets there risk being committed). The README already says
  "run from this folder so coiled-data.json and .env resolve", so the code
  should honor cwd. Let me check which shared scripts have this bug.

  Your diagnosis was right, with a twist

  It was a .env location problem — but not the one it looked like, and the 
  password was never wrong. I verified that directly: a raw ftplib login to
  ftp.ecmwf.int as ai_weather_quest with your stored password succeeded.

  The actual cause: load_dotenv() searches upward from the calling module's 
  directory — shared/ — not the cwd. So python 
  ../shared/ensemble_quintile_analysis_cli.py run from fp16FahamuAIFSv2 never
  saw your .env, AIWQ_PASSWORD stayed unset, and ftplib sent an empty password →
  530 Login authentication failed.

  (My earlier find_dotenv() spot-check was misleading: under python -c,
  co_filename is <string>, so dotenv falls back to cwd and appears to work. I
  only caught it by running a probe script from a different directory.)

  Fix: don't move the secret into shared/ (it'd risk being committed). Both CLIs
  now use find_dotenv(usecwd=True) with the old search as fallback — so "run
  from this folder", as the README instructs, actually works. Corrected the
  README too: the climatology fetch does need AIWQ_PASSWORD; it is not the
  anonymous/public download the docs claimed.

  The full local chain now runs with zero GCS

  Two new flags made it possible: --output-dir on 3a (it was deleting every
  NetCDF in cleanup, even with --no-upload) and --local-nc-dir on 3b (it
  discovered members via list_blobs, so an empty bucket meant zero members).

  On the plots

  The 6-panel check confirmed the data is intact: NH-summer 2t, Southern-Ocean
  trough in msl, ensemble mean visibly smoother than member 001, and structured
  spread (low over tropical ocean, high over land) — proving 50 genuinely
  distinct realizations. One correction to what I labelled: tp is per-6h 
  precipitation, not accumulated — it decreases in 35.4% of cells between
  consecutive steps. That matters, and it's consistent: 3b's .sum(dim='step')
  yields 2.58 mm/day global mean against ~2.7 mm/day observed.

  Committed as a04fabf. That's eight unpushed commits on main. Next would be 3c

  One thing you should know about how that went

  My first attempt hit the 2-minute shell limit (exit 143). That killed the
  wrapper, but orphaned the Python child, which kept submitting. When I
  relaunched detached, pgrep showed the orphan still alive — so for a brief
  window there were potentially two concurrent submitters, which could have
  interleaved a delete with an upload (AI-WQ overwrites via Existing file … 
  deleted. then re-upload).

  I checked the process table immediately: the orphan had already exited,
  leaving exactly one submitter, which ran all 6 to completion. The server
  listing confirms a clean end state — 6 files, one per variable×week, no
  strays. No harm done, but that was luck rather than design: I should have run
  the live submission detached from the start, as I'd done for every other long
  job today.

  fp16FahamuAIFSv2 is registered for AI Weather Quest — the submission wouldn't
  have been accepted otherwise.
